People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I’ve been feeling pretty badly about myself. Like I’ve failed, especially as a father, and as someone who’s supposed to be useful. I know that may sound dramatic, and I’m sorry, but it keeps going around in my head, and sometimes it feels like I don’t really deserve much of a future.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about yourself?\n\nPatient: Um… I’d say about a month or so. It started after my daughter left for college out of state. I mean, I’m sure lots of parents have a hard time with that, so I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but… that’s when these thoughts really started sticking around.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in the things you enjoy doing since you started feeling this way about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I mean, it’s probably not a huge thing, but I used to really look forward to working on my model railway, and watching Cubs games with my daughter was kind of our thing. Now the trains are just sitting there half-built on the table, and even turning on a game feels… pointless, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, maybe little moments here and there, but nothing I really look forward to the way I used to. The model railway used to be something I’d get kind of excited about, and Cubs games with my daughter were… special. Now the trains are just sitting there half-finished, and even putting a game on feels kind of pointless. I’m sorry, that probably sounds silly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want to worry anyone, and I’m not trying to be dramatic, but… yes, I’ve had thoughts like it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd… this is hard to say, but it scared me a little when I caught myself going over my finances, almost like I was trying to get things in order for someone else. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant loss of pleasure in most activities or a lack of reactivity to usually pleasurable stimuli nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but most days it feels like the things that used to matter just don’t really land anymore. Now the trains just sit there half-built, and even turning on a game feels pointless. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before you started feeling badly about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve definitely noticed that. I don’t know if it’s just me being lazy or out of shape, but even simple things feel strangely heavy now. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n15\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… I’m here because I’ve been feeling pretty badly about myself. Like I’ve failed, especially as a father, and as someone who’s supposed to be useful. I know that may sound dramatic, and I’m sorry, but it keeps going around in my head, and sometimes it feels like I don’t really deserve much of a future.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about yourself?\n\nPatient: Um… I’d say about a month or so. It started after my daughter left for college out of state. I mean, I’m sure lots of parents have a hard time with that, so I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but… that’s when these thoughts really started sticking around.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in the things you enjoy doing since you started feeling this way about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I mean, it’s probably not a huge thing, but I used to really look forward to working on my model railway, and watching Cubs games with my daughter was kind of our thing. Now the trains are just sitting there half-built on the table, and even turning on a game feels… pointless, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, maybe little moments here and there, but nothing I really look forward to the way I used to. The model railway used to be something I’d get kind of excited about, and Cubs games with my daughter were… special. Now the trains are just sitting there half-finished, and even putting a game on feels kind of pointless. I’m sorry, that probably sounds silly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want to worry anyone, and I’m not trying to be dramatic, but… yes, I’ve had thoughts like it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd… this is hard to say, but it scared me a little when I caught myself going over my finances, almost like I was trying to get things in order for someone else. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant loss of pleasure in most activities or a lack of reactivity to usually pleasurable stimuli nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but most days it feels like the things that used to matter just don’t really land anymore. The model railway used to be something I’d look forward to, and watching Cubs games with my daughter was… you know, our thing. Now the trains just sit there half-built, and even turning on a game feels pointless. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before you started feeling badly about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve definitely noticed that. I don’t know if it’s just me being lazy or out of shape, but even simple things feel strangely heavy now. Like carrying laundry upstairs or just sorting through the mail, it’s like I’m moving through wet cement.\n\nAnd it doesn’t really seem to matter how long I’ve been in bed. I still feel drained. I’m answering that right, I hope?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling down like this before?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anybody, but I don’t know that I’d say it was like this.\n\nOr maybe I just didn’t recognize it that way at the time. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer. This feels… different enough that I’m not sure what to compare it to.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down lasted for most of the day, every day, since it started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s been pretty much most of the day, most days. It’s been about a month now, I think—since my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nI know that probably sounds like an obvious trigger, and I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but… yeah, it’s been hanging around since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: A little, I think. I haven’t really weighed myself much, so I don’t want to overstate it, but my belt is definitely looser—I’ve had to tighten it a notch.\n\nI guess I’m just not making proper meals most days. I’ll mean to eat, and then suddenly it’s late, and I just have toast or cereal or something easy. It’s not intentional or anything… I know that’s probably not great.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual or like you can't seem to calm down and are restless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really noticed myself pacing or anything like that, and I don’t think anyone’s said I’m moving differently.\n\nMaybe I’m a bit slower getting going, but… I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I don’t want to make too much of it if it’s just me being tired or distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not sure if “worthless” is the right word, but… yes, I’ve been feeling pretty badly about myself.\n\nIt keeps coming back to this idea that I failed somehow—as a father, and just as a useful person. I know that may sound dramatic, and I’m not trying to be self-pitying, but it loops in my head a lot… like I don’t really deserve much of a future. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling down like this before, aside from the current episode?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that clearly. I mean, I’ve had low patches before, like anyone, I suppose… times where I was stressed or not myself.\n\nBut I don’t think I can point to another time that was quite like this. At least, not that I’ve recognized in that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating or making decisions since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I’ve been trying not to make too big a deal of it, but it’s been noticeable.\n\nI used to handle engineering spreadsheets without really thinking about it, and now I’ll sit there staring at something simple, like a grocery list or a bill, for half an hour and still not know what to do next. It feels embarrassing to say that, because it sounds so basic, but… yes, decisions and focusing have been harder. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or like you can't sit still since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think I’ve been pacing around or anything like that… not in a way I’d want to overstate.\n\nI suppose there are times I feel uncomfortable in my own skin, but I don’t know if that’s the same as not being able to sit still. Mostly I haven’t noticed myself being physically restless. Is that what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yes… I think I have to admit that it has affected things pretty badly.

I’m not working right now, so it’s not like I’m missing deadlines at an office, but at home, bills and house projects are just piling up because I can’t seem to make myself follow through. I keep telling myself I’ll handle it tomorrow, and then I don’t.

And socially… I’ve kind of stopped seeing the few people I used to count on, like at book club or on rides. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’m more cut off than I should be.

There’s one part I feel a little embarrassed saying… I started organizing my finances in a way that made it seem like someone else might need to understand them. That scared me. So, yes, I don’t feel safe just brushing this off anymore, if that answers your question.

I’m sorry, that probably sounds silly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want to worry anyone, and I’m not trying to be dramatic, but… yes, I’ve had thoughts like it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd… this is hard to say, but it scared me a little when I caught myself going over my finances, almost like I was trying to get things in order for someone else. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant loss of pleasure in most activities or a lack of reactivity to usually pleasurable stimuli nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but most days it feels like the things that used to matter just don’t really land anymore. The model railway used to be something I’d look forward to, and watching Cubs games with my daughter was… you know, our thing. Now the trains just sit there half-built, and even turning on a game feels pointless. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before you started feeling badly about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve definitely noticed that. I don’t know if it’s just me being lazy or out of shape, but even simple things feel strangely heavy now. Like carrying laundry upstairs or just sorting through the mail, it’s like I’m moving through wet cement.\n\nAnd it doesn’t really seem to matter how long I’ve been in bed. I still feel drained. I’m answering that right, I hope?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling down like this before?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anybody, but I don’t know that I’d say it was like this.\n\nOr maybe I just didn’t recognize it that way at the time. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer. This feels… different enough that I’m not sure what to compare it to.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down lasted for most of the day, every day, since it started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s been pretty much most of the day, most days. It’s been about a month now, I think—since my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nI know that probably sounds like an obvious trigger, and I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but… yeah, it’s been hanging around since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: A little, I think. I haven’t really weighed myself much, so I don’t want to overstate it, but my belt is definitely looser—I’ve had to tighten it a notch.\n\nI guess I’m just not making proper meals most days. I’ll mean to eat, and then suddenly it’s late, and I just have toast or cereal or something easy. It’s not intentional or anything… I know that’s probably not great.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual or like you can't seem to calm down and are restless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really noticed myself pacing or anything like that, and I don’t think anyone’s said I’m moving differently.\n\nMaybe I’m a bit slower getting going, but… I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I don’t want to make too much of it if it’s just me being tired or distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not sure if “worthless” is the right word, but… yes, I’ve been feeling pretty badly about myself.\n\nIt keeps coming back to this idea that I failed somehow—as a father, and just as a useful person. I know that may sound dramatic, and I’m not trying to be self-pitying, but it loops in my head a lot… like I don’t really deserve much of a future. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling down like this before, aside from the current episode?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that clearly. I mean, I’ve had low patches before, like anyone, I suppose… times where I was stressed or not myself.\n\nBut I don’t think I can point to another time that was quite like this. At least, not that I’ve recognized in that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating or making decisions since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I’ve been trying not to make too big a deal of it, but it’s been noticeable.\n\nI used to handle engineering spreadsheets without really thinking about it, and now I’ll sit there staring at something simple, like a grocery list or a bill, for half an hour and still not know what to do next. It feels embarrassing to say that, because it sounds so basic, but… yes, decisions and focusing have been harder. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or like you can't sit still since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think I’ve been pacing around or anything like that… not in a way I’d want to overstate.\n\nI suppose there are times I feel uncomfortable in my own skin, but I don’t know if that’s the same as not being able to sit still. Mostly I haven’t noticed myself being physically restless. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant impairment in social, occupational, or other important areas of functioning since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have to admit that it has affected things pretty badly.\n\nI’m not working right now, so it’s not like I’m missing deadlines at an office, but at home, bills and house projects are just piling up because I can’t seem to make myself follow through. I keep telling myself I’ll handle it tomorrow, and then I don’t.\n\nAnd socially… I’ve kind of stopped seeing the few people I used to count on, like at book club or on rides. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’m more cut off than I should be.\n\nThere’s one part I feel a little embarrassed saying… I started organizing my finances in a way that made it seem like someone else might need to understand them. I mean, it’s probably not a huge thing, but I used to really look forward to working on my model railway, and watching Cubs games with my daughter was kind of our thing. Now the trains are just sitting there half-built on the table, and even turning on a game feels… pointless, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, maybe little moments here and there, but nothing I really look forward to the way I used to. The model railway used to be something I’d get kind of excited about, and Cubs games with my daughter were… special. Now the trains are just sitting there half-finished, and even putting a game on feels kind of pointless. I’m sorry, that probably sounds silly.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts about harming yourself?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t want to worry anyone, and I’m not trying to be dramatic, but… yes, I’ve had thoughts like it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up.\n\nAnd… this is hard to say, but it scared me a little when I caught myself going over my finances, almost like I was trying to get things in order for someone else. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant loss of pleasure in most activities or a lack of reactivity to usually pleasurable stimuli nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but most days it feels like the things that used to matter just don’t really land anymore. The model railway used to be something I’d look forward to, and watching Cubs games with my daughter was… you know, our thing. Now the trains just sit there half-built, and even turning on a game feels pointless. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before you started feeling badly about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve definitely noticed that. I don’t know if it’s just me being lazy or out of shape, but even simple things feel strangely heavy now. Like carrying laundry upstairs or just sorting through the mail, it’s like I’m moving through wet cement.\n\nAnd it doesn’t really seem to matter how long I’ve been in bed. I still feel drained. I’m answering that right, I hope?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling down like this before?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anybody, but I don’t know that I’d say it was like this.\n\nOr maybe I just didn’t recognize it that way at the time. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer. This feels… different enough that I’m not sure what to compare it to.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down lasted for most of the day, every day, since it started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s been pretty much most of the day, most days. It’s been about a month now, I think—since my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nI know that probably sounds like an obvious trigger, and I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but… yeah, it’s been hanging around since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: A little, I think. I haven’t really weighed myself much, so I don’t want to overstate it, but my belt is definitely looser—I’ve had to tighten it a notch.\n\nI guess I’m just not making proper meals most days. I’ll mean to eat, and then suddenly it’s late, and I just have toast or cereal or something easy. It’s not intentional or anything… I know that’s probably not great.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual or like you can't seem to calm down and are restless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really noticed myself pacing or anything like that, and I don’t think anyone’s said I’m moving differently.\n\nMaybe I’m a bit slower getting going, but… I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I don’t want to make too much of it if it’s just me being tired or distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not sure if “worthless” is the right word, but… yes, I’ve been feeling pretty badly about myself.\n\nIt keeps coming back to this idea that I failed somehow—as a father, and just as a useful person. I know that may sound dramatic, and I’m not trying to be self-pitying, but it loops in my head a lot… like I don’t really deserve much of a future. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling down like this before, aside from the current episode?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that clearly. I mean, I’ve had low patches before, like anyone, I suppose… times where I was stressed or not myself.\n\nBut I don’t think I can point to another time that was quite like this. At least, not that I’ve recognized in that way. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any difficulty concentrating or making decisions since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I’ve been trying not to make too big a deal of it, but it’s been noticeable.\n\nI used to handle engineering spreadsheets without really thinking about it, and now I’ll sit there staring at something simple, like a grocery list or a bill, for half an hour and still not know what to do next. It feels embarrassing to say that, because it sounds so basic, but… yes, decisions and focusing have been harder. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or like you can't sit still since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think I’ve been pacing around or anything like that… not in a way I’d want to overstate.\n\nI suppose there are times I feel uncomfortable in my own skin, but I don’t know if that’s the same as not being able to sit still. Mostly I haven’t noticed myself being physically restless. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant impairment in social, occupational, or other important areas of functioning since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think I have to admit that it has affected things pretty badly.\n\nI’m not working right now, so it’s not like I’m missing deadlines at an office, but at home, bills and house projects are just piling up because I can’t seem to make myself follow through. I keep telling myself I’ll handle it tomorrow, and then I don’t.\n\nAnd socially… I’ve kind of stopped seeing the few people I used to count on, like at book club or on rides. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I’m more cut off than I should be.\n\nThere’s one part I feel a little embarrassed saying… I started organizing my finances in a way that made it seem like someone else might need to understand them. That scared me. So, yes, I don’t feel safe just brushing this off anymore, if that answers your question.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about how you would end your life if you were to act on those thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I had a clear plan, exactly. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is.\n\nBut I have had thoughts like… it might be easier if I just didn’t wake up. And what scared me was that I caught myself going over my finances almost like I was trying to make things understandable for someone else, if they had to deal with them.\n\nThat felt different from just a passing thought. I’m embarrassed saying that, but… yes, that’s been there. Is that what you’re asking?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I’m not completely sure how to answer that one. I don’t think I’ve been noticeably slowed down or agitated nearly every day, at least not in a way I could point to clearly.

I mean, I may sit around more than I should, but I don’t know if that’s the same thing you mean clinically. I haven’t had people telling me I’m moving strangely or anything. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?

It started after my daughter left for college out of state. I mean, it’s probably not a huge thing, but I used to really look forward to working on my model railway, and watching Cubs games with my daughter was kind of our thing. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant loss of pleasure in most activities or a lack of reactivity to usually pleasurable stimuli nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but most days it feels like the things that used to matter just don’t really land anymore. The model railway used to be something I’d look forward to, and watching Cubs games with my daughter was… you know, our thing. Now the trains just sit there half-built, and even turning on a game feels pointless. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before you started feeling badly about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve definitely noticed that. I don’t know if it’s just me being lazy or out of shape, but even simple things feel strangely heavy now. Like carrying laundry upstairs or just sorting through the mail, it’s like I’m moving through wet cement.\n\nAnd it doesn’t really seem to matter how long I’ve been in bed. I still feel drained. I’m answering that right, I hope?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling down like this before?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anybody, but I don’t know that I’d say it was like this.\n\nOr maybe I just didn’t recognize it that way at the time. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer. This feels… different enough that I’m not sure what to compare it to.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down lasted for most of the day, every day, since it started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s been pretty much most of the day, most days. It’s been about a month now, I think—since my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nI know that probably sounds like an obvious trigger, and I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but… yeah, it’s been hanging around since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: A little, I think. I haven’t really weighed myself much, so I don’t want to overstate it, but my belt is definitely looser—I’ve had to tighten it a notch.\n\nI guess I’m just not making proper meals most days. I’ll mean to eat, and then suddenly it’s late, and I just have toast or cereal or something easy. I don’t want to make too much of it if it’s just me being tired or distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not sure if “worthless” is the right word, but… yes, I’ve been feeling pretty badly about myself.\n\nIt keeps coming back to this idea that I failed somehow—as a father, and just as a useful person. I know that may sound dramatic, and I’m not trying to be self-pitying, but it loops in my head a lot… like I don’t really deserve much of a future. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling down like this before, aside from the current episode?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that clearly. So, yes, I don’t feel safe just brushing this off anymore, if that answers your question.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about how you would end your life if you were to act on those thoughts about not waking up?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t think I had a clear plan, exactly. I haven’t had people telling me I’m moving strangely or anything. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking about?\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you experienced at least five of the symptoms we've discussed nearly every day since you started feeling this way?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 51 / not_in_workforce / graduate\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m… not totally sure how to count them, to be honest.

It feels like a lot of this has been there most days, but I don’t want to overstate it or say the wrong thing. If you’re asking whether it’s been enough symptoms happening nearly every day… I think you’d probably have to help me sort through that. I know I haven’t felt like myself, but I’m not sure I can confidently say the number.

It started after my daughter left for college out of state. I mean, I’m sure lots of parents have a hard time with that, so I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is, but… that’s when these thoughts really started sticking around.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in the things you enjoy doing since you started feeling this way about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I have. I mean, it’s probably not a huge thing, but I used to really look forward to working on my model railway, and watching Cubs games with my daughter was kind of our thing. Now the trains are just sitting there half-built on the table, and even turning on a game feels… pointless, I guess. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: I wish I could say yes. I mean, maybe little moments here and there, but nothing I really look forward to the way I used to. The model railway used to be something I’d get kind of excited about, and Cubs games with my daughter were… special. Now the trains are just sitting there half-finished, and even putting a game on feels kind of pointless. I don’t know if that’s the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a significant loss of pleasure in most activities or a lack of reactivity to usually pleasurable stimuli nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s fair to say. I mean, I don’t want to overstate it, but most days it feels like the things that used to matter just don’t really land anymore. The model railway used to be something I’d look forward to, and watching Cubs games with my daughter was… you know, our thing. Now the trains just sit there half-built, and even turning on a game feels pointless. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or how quickly you move compared to before you started feeling badly about yourself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’ve definitely noticed that. I don’t know if it’s just me being lazy or out of shape, but even simple things feel strangely heavy now. Like carrying laundry upstairs or just sorting through the mail, it’s like I’m moving through wet cement.\n\nAnd it doesn’t really seem to matter how long I’ve been in bed. I still feel drained. I’m answering that right, I hope?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods of feeling down like this before?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anybody, but I don’t know that I’d say it was like this.\n\nOr maybe I just didn’t recognize it that way at the time. I don’t want to give you the wrong answer. This feels… different enough that I’m not sure what to compare it to.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being down lasted for most of the day, every day, since it started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I’d say it’s been pretty much most of the day, most days. It’s been about a month now, I think—since my daughter left for college out of state.\n\nI know that probably sounds like an obvious trigger, and I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but… yeah, it’s been hanging around since then.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: A little, I think. I haven’t really weighed myself much, so I don’t want to overstate it, but my belt is definitely looser—I’ve had to tighten it a notch.\n\nI guess I’m just not making proper meals most days. I’ll mean to eat, and then suddenly it’s late, and I just have toast or cereal or something easy. It’s not intentional or anything… I know that’s probably not great.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual or like you can't seem to calm down and are restless?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I haven’t really noticed myself pacing or anything like that, and I don’t think anyone’s said I’m moving differently.\n\nMaybe I’m a bit slower getting going, but… I don’t know if that’s what you mean. I don’t want to make too much of it if it’s just me being tired or distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I’m not sure if “worthless” is the right word, but… yes, I’ve been feeling pretty badly about myself.\n\nIt keeps coming back to this idea that I failed somehow—as a father, and just as a useful person. I know that may sound dramatic, and I’m not trying to be self-pitying, but it loops in my head a lot… like I don’t really deserve much of a future. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of feeling down like this before, aside from the current episode?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know how to answer that clearly. At least, not that I’ve recognized in that way. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt restless or like you can't sit still since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I don’t think I’ve been pacing around or anything like that… not in a way I’d want to overstate.\n\nI suppose there are times I feel uncomfortable in my own skin, but I don’t know if that’s the same as not being able to sit still. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think so. I’ve been trying to tell myself I’m just behind or being lazy, but it’s gotten to where bills and things around the house are piling up because I just can’t seem to follow through.

And I’ve kind of stopped seeing the few people I used to see — like at book club, or on rides. I don’t mean I’ve cut everyone off dramatically, but I’m not showing up the way I used to.

I guess the part that makes me feel like I shouldn’t just brush it off is… when I started organizing my finances like someone else might need to understand them. That scared me. So yes, it is interfering most of the time, I think.

I’ve been trying to tell myself I’m just behind or being lazy, but it’s gotten to where bills and things around the house are piling up because I just can’t seem to follow through.\n\nAnd I’ve kind of stopped seeing the few people I used to see — like at book club, or on rides.
